The record is reshaped in spec-legal ways before `append_many` writes it: optional payload fields left out (the MAY rows of each RFC table), a string `chat` (SPEC §3.2), a `tz` the host's zone database lacks (§2), a day with only tier-3 lines; and an empty month file after. Eight short cases run in CI; the long ones are `slow` (LOGBOOK_SLOW=1). A failure names the seed, the days, the reshapings and the command.

### Housekeeping
- Changelog fragments: a pull request adds `changelog.d/<short-slug>.md` instead of a line in CHANGELOG.md, so parallel pull requests no longer conflict on it. At release time `scripts/changelog_assemble.py X.Y.Z` (stdlib only) folds the fragments, and the `Unreleased` block while one exists, into the new version section and deletes them. The `changelog` check fails a pull request that edits CHANGELOG.md or adds no fragment; the `no-changelog` label opts out. The release checklist is `docs/releasing.md`.
- Property tests of the format (`tests/properties/`, the October 2026 audit): what SPEC.md guarantees, stated for any input and searched for a counterexample by Hypothesis — canonicalisation (§3, RFC 8785: idempotent, key order and stored whitespace aside, injective on values, `1` and `1.0` one number), the hash chain (§1, §3, §6: any sequence verifies, an append changes only the head, any byte flip of any line is rejected naming the line, a truncation reports the last good head), tiers (§4, ADR 0016: an export at tier t is exactly the lines at or below it, tier 1 carries no profile of tier 2 or 3 as SPEC.md's own table lists them, export then import is idempotent on the head), time (§2, §3.2: the day of a line is the local date in the record's zone, midnight turns the day, a DST transition leaves no instant without a day or with two, a duration is the real seconds elapsed), identity (RFC 0006: every spelling of a number or an address is one ref and one person, two identifiers never collide) and the attachment store (§1.1: store then load is the same bytes under their SHA-256, storing twice does not duplicate). The suite's Hypothesis profile is deterministic. The example tests these properties make redundant are gone. One property is marked `xfail(strict=True)`: a month file cut inside a line makes `verify` report seq 0 and GENESIS, not the head of the lines before the cut.
- A pull request that renames a command or changes a reader's output runs `LOGBOOK_SLOW=1 uv run pytest tests/test_fuzz_readers.py` once before it merges (CLAUDE.md, CONTRIBUTING.md and a box in the pull request template): the fuzz readers are slow-tier, which the pull-request CI tier skips, so #215 turned `main` red on 4 October and #217 fixed it forward.
- The test suite imports the 0.6 paths (`logbook.core.store`, `logbook.contrib.adapters`, …), never the 0.5 ones
  `logbook._shim` keeps alive, and in the suite the shim's DeprecationWarning is an error, so no test drifts back
  to an old path; `scripts/rewrite_test_imports.py` (`layout.MOVED`, re-runnable, `--check`) is what moved them.
  The shims and `docs/migration-0.6.md` are unchanged.
- The ruff hook in `.pre-commit-config.yaml` is the version CI runs (`uv.lock`), so the two agree on import order.

### Profiles and spec
- `commitment/v1` and `commitment-close/v1` (RFC 0007) are withdrawn by RFC 0031: nothing ever wrote or read them.
  They leave the tier table of SPEC §4 and the seed list in `rfcs/README.md`, and the promises reader's documents
  state their own rules instead of citing RFC 0007. No line, reader or command changes.
- `trip/v1` is written as `journey/v1` for new lines (RFC 0031, ADR 0021): `easypark`, `sbb` and `passages` write `kind`
  `journey`, `schema` `journey/v1`; `show` formats both spellings the same way and `search --kinds trip` or `--kinds journey`
  finds both. Nothing is migrated: a `trip/v1` line already in a record stays as it is. RFC 0020 carries the amendment and
  is marked *parked · may change before v1.0*.
- One conformance fixture per frozen payload profile under `conformance/profiles/<profile>/` (RFC 0031, SPEC §6.1): a one-line
  record, the line's canonical form and hash, the `show` row and the `day` and `days` JSON, generated by
  `conformance/make_profiles.py` and held byte for byte by the tests. The nineteen RFCs say *frozen for v1.0* in their status lines.

## 0.5.0 — 2026-10-01

### Profiles and spec
- `apple-health`: resting heart rate and HRV are taken as the store keeps them — count/min and ms — no longer multiplied by 60 and 1,000 (a real store showed resting rates in the thousands); heart rate keeps its ×60 (the store holds count/s, the original count/min, checked row for row). Every mapped type's stored unit is now recorded in RFC 0014 ("Units as the store keeps them"), with what a record imported before this version shows and how to correct it.
- `stats --health`: a night's hours are the union of one device's asleep spans (`asleep`, `core`, `deep`, `rem`), then the longest device; a source that writes the same night twice no longer doubles it (a real record showed 11–14 h nights), and devices are never summed. RFC 0014 rule 4 says so.
- `derive stays`: a tracker that is silent while the owner is still (Dawarich) no longer breaks a stay into pieces — a gap whose next point is back inside the radius continues the stay whatever its length, so the `move 10 m · gap` rows are gone; a stay lasts until the tracker's next point when that comes after a silence of `merge_gap_s` or more and lies within a walk of `merge_gap_s` (`walk_max_kmh`, 1.2 km by default), so an evening at home with no points until morning is the night, not in transit, even when the first point of the morning is after the night window and already away; the reading hands the derivation the first point after the window for that. `merge_gap_s` now governs only an excursion: out of the radius and back within it is one stay. Settings note in the README and the module.
- `logbook repair health-units [--dry-run]` (`logbook/repair.py`): the migration for a record whose `apple-health` lines carry resting heart rate and HRV in the wrong unit — per standing wrong line a corrected line (`value` divided by 60 or 1,000, `raw_id` suffixed `:u2`, `payload.supersedes` the old id) and a retraction (`source` `logbook`); idempotent; `--dry-run` prints counts and writes nothing. RFC 0014 adds `supersedes` to the profile and documents the migration; README "Upgrading" says when to run it.
- RFC 0024, payload profile `keeper/v1` (draft): one line per photo the captain marked a keeper — `photo` (`{line, asset_id, library, file_name?}`, the photo/v1 line it points at), `at` (the photo's capture time, so the keeper is on the photo's day), `lane` (`memory`, a day's hero photo, or `art`), `source` (where the mark was made: `ios-photos`, `immich`, `manual`), `raw_id` `<photo line id>:<lane>`. A keeper is the owner's act, never a reader's guess; unmarking is a retraction; a day's hero photos are its keepers.
- Conformance: `conformance/sample-logbook` grows from 16 to 31 lines — the first sixteen unchanged, then one synthetic line of every profile added since v0.2, fourteen on the Sunday: a location with a `subject` (the boat), `health-sample`, `call`, `browse`, `watch`, `task`, `mail` (an attachment by digest, no `path`), `listen`, `trip` (a parking session with a price, tier 3), `transaction`, `flight` (evidence `tracked`), `highlight`, `voice-memo` (audio by digest, not stored) and `crossing`, then a `keeper` (RFC 0024) on the first photo's day. `expected.json` names the new head, `035a74e0…`; the conformance README keeps the table of heads per release. `make_sample.py` writes bytes (no CRLF on Windows) and leaves the policies `init` writes out of the fixture.
- SPEC §4 gains the table of default tiers per payload profile, repeating each RFC as merged (tier 1: location, photo, event, crossing, call, flight, trip, migration; tier 2: retraction, transcript, resolution, commitment, message, note, mail, task, browse, watch, listen, highlight, voice-memo; tier 3: health-sample, transaction), with MUST marked where the RFC says so and the SHOULD/`--tier` rule in one sentence; §5 points at `rfcs/README.md` for the list instead of the seven seed profiles. One disagreement, resolved in favour of the RFC: RFC 0004 says `transcript/v1` SHOULD be tier 2, while RFC 0012 called it tier 3 and the `transcript`, `granola` and `wispr-flow` adapters write 3. The table says 2, RFC 0012's aside now cites RFC 0004, and the adapters keep writing 3 as a SHOULD allows a more careful producer (`--tier 2` follows the RFC); no line's tier changes.
- RFC 0014 `health-sample/v1`: types `body_fat_pct` (%), `fat_mass`, `fat_free_mass`, `muscle_mass`, `bone_mass`, `body_water` (kg), `bmi` (kg/m2), `vo2_max` (mL/kg/min), `energy_intake` (kcal); rules 9 (body composition is one line per quantity), 10 (a logged food is `energy_intake`; a day without a clock is a span) and 11 (a reading relayed from another app is skipped); a meal is now a health sample only as its energy.
- RFC 0002 `photo/v1`: `favorite`, `hidden` and `albums` fields; a provenance rule for a library that records how an asset arrived; and the merge rule for one asset seen by several libraries — the same original `file_name` (ignoring case) and capture times within one second, folded by a reader as a union, never rewritten by an adapter.
- RFC 0019, payload profile `listen/v1` (draft): one line per listen — `media` `track`/`episode`, `title`, `artist` or `show` (and `publisher`) as the service spells them, never resolved, `album`, `url`, `duration_s` when known, `played_s` when the source keeps a playhead, `published`, `service`; `raw_id` the service's id with the time as spelled, so a track identified twice is two listens; a zoneless time (Shazam) is read in the record's zone. Tier 2. Listed in `rfcs/README.md`.
- RFC 0018, payload profile `watch/v1` (draft): one line per video watched or search made — `raw_id` `<service>:<time as the export spells it>:<sha256(url, else title)[:16]>`, `action` `watched`/`searched`, `title` (a search's query), `url`, `video_id`, `channel` `{name, url?}` never resolved, `service` `youtube`/`youtube-music`/…; an advertisement is never a line. Tier 2. Listed in `rfcs/README.md`.
- RFC 0017, payload profile `browse/v1` (draft): one line per page opened, bookmarked or saved — `raw_id` `<app>:<time as the source spells it>:<sha256(url)[:16]>` (no browser keeps a visit id across exports), `url`, `title`, `action` `visit`/`bookmark`/`save`, `browser` (the app that recorded it), `transition` (Chrome's `page_transition` lower-cased), `folder` for a bookmark, `tags` and `status` for a read-later save. Tier 2: reading is not public activity. Listed in `rfcs/README.md`.
- RFC 0016, payload profile `task/v1` (draft), written for every task app at once: one line per task — `raw_id` `<source id>@<modified_at>` (a snapshot per edit, as `note/v1`), `title`, `status` one of `open`/`done`/`cancelled` whatever the app calls them, `due` as a day (`YYYY-MM-DD`) when the source keeps a day only else an RFC 3339 instant, `completed_at`, `list`, `notes`, `parent` (a subtask is its own line), `priority` `low`/`medium`/`high`, `tags`, `recurrence` as RRULE text, `modified_at`, `url`; `at` the creation time when the source keeps it, else the last modification. Apple Reminders and TickTick fit the same fields. Listed in `rfcs/README.md`.
- RFC 0020 `trip/v1`: one movement or metered stop by a paid mobility service — `mode` `ride`, `parking`, `rental` or `transit`, `provider`, `from` and `to` as the service's own place (name, address, code, operator, coordinates; never resolved), `distance_m`, `price`, `status`. A parking session is a trip with one place; a service that keeps only places last used writes a line with no end and `extra.observed` `last_used`. Producers: `easypark` (below); `uber` has none yet — the Uber app's container in an iOS backup holds saved places and the app's analytics, not the ride history, so the mapping waits for Uber's data export.
- RFC 0021, payload profile `transaction/v1` (draft): one line per movement of money as a financial app recorded it — `raw_id` the app's own id; `amount` signed from the owner's side (negative when money left, positive when it arrived), a number as the source stores it; `currency` (ISO 4217); `merchant` as the app shows the other side, never resolved; `category` as the app spells its own; `date` the day the app files it under; `account`; `provider` the app; `status` `pending`/`posted`; `note`; the app's type, recurring flag, original strings, a shared expense's total and member shares under `extra`. Kind `transaction`, tier 3 always (SPEC §4: money; `--tier` never lowers it). Listed in `rfcs/README.md`. Of the money apps a phone backup holds, Copilot keeps its whole transaction table and Splitwise every expense with its shares; Revolut keeps no transactions on the phone (profile, market news, a chat), so it has no adapter from a backup.
- RFC 0015, payload profile `mail/v1` (draft): one line per email message — `raw_id` the `Message-ID` without its brackets, prefixed by the mailbox address and a colon when the source knows it, else `sha256:<digest of the message bytes>` for a message with none; `message_id`; `thread` the first entry of `References`, else `In-Reply-To`, else the message's own id; `from`, `to`, `cc`, `bcc` as `{email, name?}` with the address lower-cased, the same identity a `resolution/v1` email ref names and never resolved here; `subject`; `date` as RFC 3339 with the header's own offset (`at` is the same instant in UTC, `tz` the record's zone: a `Date` header has an offset, not a zone name); `direction` `sent`/`received` by the owner's addresses; `labels` as the mailbox spells them; `body` as plain text (`text/plain`, else `text/html` stripped, `extra.body_from: html`); `size`; `attachments` as `{filename, media_type, sha256, bytes}` — the SPEC §1.1 shape plus the file name, `path` only when the bytes are in the store — and `account`, `extra.gmail_thread_id`, `extra.in_reply_to`, `extra.references`, `extra.decoding_errors`. Tier 2 (SPEC §4: personal mail), lowered or raised only for a whole import. Listed in `rfcs/README.md`.
- RFC 0014, payload profile `health-sample/v1` (draft): one line per measurement — `type` (`steps`, `distance`, `active_energy`, `basal_energy`, `flights_climbed`, `heart_rate`, `resting_hr`, `hrv`, `weight`, `sleep` with `stage`, `workout`), `value`, a fixed `unit` per type, `device` as the store identifies it, `source_name` when the store names it; `kind` `health`, tier 3 (SPEC §4; `logbook add --tier` overrides). High-frequency counts are summed into 15-minute UTC-aligned buckets per device (`raw_id` `<type>:<bucket start>:<device>`), heart rate is capped at one reading per minute per device, sleep stages and workouts are spans; a line never sums across devices, a reader takes the larger device per bucket. Any other line is one store row, `raw_id` `<type>:<data_id>`, so a re-import appends nothing (ADR 0017).
- RFC 0001 `location/v1` amended, backward compatibly: an optional `subject`, the id of an asset in `assets.json`, absent meaning the owner; an asset's positions are its own track and a reader never merges them with the owner's. SPEC §3.2's run rule names the subject (no format bump, no hash change). ADR 0018, assets are subjects, not places: "aboard" is derived downstream, never written on a line.
- RFC 0013, payload profile `flight/v1` (draft): one flight the owner was on — `date`, `carrier` (IATA), `number`, `from`/`to` as `{iata, icao}`, scheduled and actual gate times (take-off and landing too when a source keeps them), `aircraft` `{type, registration}`, `role` (`passenger`|`pilot`), `cancelled`, `diverted_to` — and how the three producers meet: `evidence` is `tracked` (a flight tracker's export), `inferred` (the record's own calendar and location lines) or `declared` (the owner's word). A flight is keyed by `(date, carrier, number)`; every producer observation is a line, and one that arrives for a flight already standing supersedes it with the merge — tracked over inferred over declared, field by field, the declared `role` always — listing every folded-in line under `observations` (`logbook/flights.py`: `build`, `key`, `standing`, `merge`, `reconcile`). Tier 1; a PNR is never kept. Two tables ship in the package: `logbook/tables/airports.csv` (OurAirports, public domain: the 1,150 large airports with scheduled service, IATA, ICAO, name, coordinates and an IANA zone assigned by `scripts/make_airports.py`, the only flights code that touches the network and only at development time) and `logbook/tables/airlines.csv` (IATA ↔ ICAO designators, so Flighty's `SWR` and a calendar's `LX 561` are one flight). `--airports FILE` on `add` and `infer`, or `LOGBOOK_AIRPORTS`, adds a private field or overrides a row (same columns; `icao` and `name` optional).
- SPEC clarifications, round two, no format bump, no hash change (#84): a reader other than `verify` MAY rely on month-file placement; timestamps compare as instants, not text; `tz` names no edition of the zone database and an unknown zone never invalidates a record; a line whose payload does not match its profile is valid and a reader MUST NOT fail on it; the conformance sample tests the chain, not the profiles. New §3.2 *Readers*: a day is ordered by instant then `seq`; retraction lines are not listed on their own day; an alias walk that ends without an entity line names nothing (RFC 0006 rule 6 reworded to match); a collapsed run of points ends at the last point's `end`, else its `at`; read commands MAY refuse a `logbook/0.1` record. Found by logbook-ts while building its `show`. `CONTRIBUTING.md` rewritten: one issue per PR, signed and signed-off commits, synthetic data only, green CI, ADR 0017 for adapters.
- RFC 0012, payload profile `call/v1` (draft): one line per call in a call log — `raw_id` the store's own id, `direction` (`incoming`/`outgoing`), `answered`, `duration_s`, `counterparty` as a source-native `{kind: phone|email|handle, value}` ref (RFC 0006, never resolved here), `service` as the source names it (`cellular`, `facetime`, `facetime-audio`, `whatsapp`, …); `at` the start, `end` the start plus the duration when answered. Tier 1: a call record is metadata about the owner's own time, like a calendar attendee, and carries no one's words. Listed in `rfcs/README.md`.
- RFC 0006: a resolution line may carry `alias_of` — a ref `{ kind, value }` — instead of `entity`: this ref and that ref are the same identity, and the line mints nothing. Rule 6: a reader follows `alias_of` to the target ref's own last line standing, at most 4 hops, stopping on a cycle or at a ref with no line standing, and the line it stops on decides; retraction and last-wins apply to an alias exactly as to an entity line. `logbook/resolve.py` does this, so `show` names a ref that is an alias of a resolved phone without any change of its own (#58).

### Adapters and import-backup
- `sbb` file adapter: SBB Mobile's `SbbMobile.db` (`TicketGroups` with the validity window, the stations on the ticket, the ticket type, the head count and the order item ids as JSON; `PurchasedTickets` for the price, class, product and refund state) and `ch.sbb.coredata.pasttrips.sqlite` (`ZMYTRIP.ZVERBINDUNG`, the app's JSON of a saved journey whose day has passed: stations, Swiss-time clocks, transfers, real-time clocks, legs with coordinates and `IC 8`-style line names) → `trip/v1` (RFC 0020), mode `transit`, provider `sbb`, `tz` `Europe/Zurich`: one line per ticket group (`raw_id` `ticket:<groupId>`, `extra.observed` `ticket`, `at`/`end` the validity window, `price` `{amount, currency}` summed over the group's tickets, `status` `cancelled` when all were refunded, tier 3 with a price else 1) and one per journey (`raw_id` `trip:<verbindungId>`, `extra.observed` `journey`, planned clocks as `at`/`end`, the real ones under `extra.actual_*`, `extra.legs` with `mode` from the app's icon — `train`, `bus`, `tram`, `ferry`, `walk` — and `line`, `transfers`, `international`). Either store brings the other beside it. The traveller's name, date of birth, QR code, rendered ticket and order reference are never read. `show` prints `From → To, transit, sbb, 58.00 CHF, 1 change`. RFC 0020's notes gain the ticket-versus-journey sentence.
- `import-backup`: `sbb` runs last: `SbbMobile.db` from the shared container with the past-trips store from the app's own container copied beside it — the first companion from another domain.
- `withings` file adapter: the Withings app's per-profile stores → `health-sample/v1` (RFC 0014), tier 3. `<profile>_WTHealth.sqlite` (`ZWTOBJECT` with `WTQuantityType.*` identifiers and Apple-epoch dates → `ZWTQUANTITY`, `ZWTSOURCE` for the device model and the relaying app's bundle id, `ZWTSEARCHABLEMETADATA` for the zone) gives one line per quantity of a standing on the scale — `weight`, `body_fat_pct`, `fat_mass`, `fat_free_mass`, `muscle_mass`, `bone_mass`, `body_water`, `bmi`, `vo2_max` — all at the standing's instant; `<profile>_Measure.sqlite` (`ZWTSPECIALIZEDGROUP` typed by its entity name in `Z_PRIMARYKEY` → `ZWTMEASUREGROUP` for date, zone and device, `ZWTMEASURE` mantissa × 10^exponent) gives `heart_rate` readings. Rows relayed from another app (a watch via Apple Health, Garmin) are skipped and counted `skipped_relayed`; removed rows, rows without a value (correlations), without a date, and types outside the profile (impedance, scores, height, blood pressure) are counted too. `raw_id` is `<type>:<profile>:<local id>`, `device` the model number, `extra.profile` the profile. Either store brings its partner of the same profile; a folder brings every profile. `logbook add withings <store-or-folder>`.
- `myfitnesspal` file adapter: MyFitnessPal's `maindb.sqlite` → `health-sample/v1`, tier 3: `food_entries` × `food_portions` × `nutritional_values` (nutrient 0) → one `energy_intake` line per logged food in kcal (meal name from `meal_names`, food, brand, quantity and portion under `extra`), at the entry's local clock in the store's `timezone_identifier` (else the record's zone); `measurements` of type Weight → `weight` (pounds converted when the owner's unit preference says so, the entered value under `extra.original`); `exercise_entries` → `workout` with the duration as `value` and the exercise's name and calories under `extra`. An entry the app keeps only the day of is a span over that local day with `extra.all_day`. Daily step totals, the other body measurements, zero weights and exercise without a duration are skipped and counted. `logbook add myfitnesspal <store>`.
- `import-backup`: `withings` (the `coredata/` folder's `*_WTHealth.sqlite` glob with `*_Measure.sqlite` as companions, every profile, the adapter run on the folder) and `myfitnesspal` run after `apple-photos`.
- `apple-photos` file adapter: an iPhone's `Photos.sqlite` (`ZASSET` with Apple-epoch capture dates, kind and subtype, favourite, hidden and trashed flags, coordinates with `-180` for none; `ZADDITIONALASSETATTRIBUTES` for the original file name, the zone name and how the asset was imported; `ZGENERICALBUM` kind 2 for the owner's own albums through the `Z_<n>ASSETS` join table, found by its shape because `n` changes with iOS; `ZDETECTEDFACE` → `ZPERSON`) → one `photo/v1` line per asset not in the trash, tier 1: `asset_id`/`raw_id` the UUID, `library` `apple-photos`, `file_name` the original name, `media`, `lat`/`lon`, `width`/`height`, `duration_s`, `live_photo`, `provenance` (`screenshot` from the subtype, `camera` from a live photo, a camera video kind or an import by the camera, `received` from another app or AirDrop, else `other`), `faces`, `people` the UUIDs of the people the owner named (a merged person under its target; ids, never names), `favorite`, `hidden`, `albums` sorted; `extra` the UTI and the importing bundle. `tz` is the asset's IANA zone name when it has one (`GMT+0200` is not one). Trashed and undated assets skipped and counted. `logbook add photos <store>`.
- `import-backup`: `apple-photos` runs after `voice-memos`, on a copy of `Photos.sqlite` (with its -wal) from `CameraRollDomain`; no image is copied.
- `voice-memos` file adapter and RFC 0023 `voice-memo/v1`: Voice Memos' `CloudRecordings.db` (`ZCLOUDRECORDING` with Apple-epoch dates, the owner's title in `ZCUSTOMLABEL`, the file name in `ZPATH`) → one `voice-memo/v1` line per recording, tier 2: `raw_id` the recording's unique id, `title`, `duration_s`, `file_name`, `end` = `at` + duration, and `media` the audio as a SPEC §1.1 reference — `.m4a` as `audio/mp4`, the app's `.qta` composition as `video/quicktime` — hashed a chunk at a time from `Recordings/` beside the store (an `import-backup` copy) or beside the store itself (the phone's and the Mac's layout). Without `--attachments` the reference has no `path` and the bytes stay where they are (counted *attachments referenced, not stored*); with it each file goes through the new streamed `Logbook.attach_file` (`attachments.write_path`, `reference_path`, `digest_path`: a hundred-megabyte recording is never held whole), and a re-run with the flag appends nothing but fills the store. A recording whose file is missing is a line without media, counted. No transcription (RFC 0023 rule 3). `show` prints `Title (m:ss)`, then `audio missing` or `not stored`.
- `import-backup`: `voice-memos` runs after `apple-books`; only the `.m4a`/`.qta` files under `Recordings/` travel with the store (`media_suffixes`), not the waveforms and composition manifests. `--attachments` stores their audio. An adapter whose `run` takes `store_file` gets `Logbook.attach_file`.
- `apple-books` file adapter and RFC 0022 `highlight/v1`: Apple Books' `AEAnnotation*.sqlite` (`ZAEANNOTATION` with Apple-epoch dates; a row with selected text is a highlight, its colour index and underline flag under `extra`; a row without text is a bookmark, except the type-3 row that marks where the owner last stopped reading, which is the app's state and is skipped and counted) → one `highlight/v1` line per mark, tier 2: `raw_id` the annotation UUID, `type`, `quote` and `note` verbatim, `location` the EPUB CFI as text, `modified_at` when later than `at`. The title and author come from the `BKLibrary*.sqlite` beside the store or in Books' own `../BKLibrary/` folder, by asset id; a book the library does not list leaves `asset_id` and no title, counted. Deleted marks (tombstones), undated and placeholder-dated rows are skipped and counted. `logbook add books <store-or-folder>` (`books` is an alias; a folder is searched for the store). `show` prints a highlight as `“quote” — Title · note` and a bookmark as `bookmark — Title @ location`.
- `import-backup`: `apple-books` runs after `twitter`. A `Source` can now list `companions` — other files, by path or glob and from any domain, copied beside the store under their own names with their `-wal`/`-shm` (Books' library, so the adapter finds the titles beside the copy) — and keep only media with given `media_suffixes`.
- `pocket` file adapter: a Pocket CSV export (`title,url,time_added,tags,status`, one part or the unzipped folder of parts) → `browse/v1`, one `save` per row, source `pocket`, `browser` `pocket`, `raw_id` `pocket:<time_added>:<sha256(url)[:16]>`, `tags` split on `|`, `status` `unread`/`archived`. Decided against `note/v1`: a note is the owner's own words (RFC 0010 rule 4) and a saved page is not; a bookmark already lives in `browse/v1`, and a read-later save is a bookmark kept in another app (RFC 0017, notes). A row without a url or a time is skipped and counted.
- `shazam` file adapter: the app's library export (`shazamlibrary.csv`: a `Shazam Library` title line, then `Index,TagTime,Title,Artist,URL,TrackKey`) → `listen/v1`, one `track` per row, `raw_id` `shazam:<TrackKey>:<TagTime>`, `extra.track_key`; a row without a time or a title is skipped and counted. Sniffed by the title line or the header.
- `apple-podcasts` adapter, file and live (ADR 0017): Apple Podcasts' `MTLibrary.sqlite` (`ZMTEPISODE` joined to `ZMTPODCAST`; `ZLASTDATEPLAYED` seconds since 2001; the columns discovered with `PRAGMA table_info` so only `ZTITLE`, `ZUUID` and `ZLASTDATEPLAYED` are required and a store with other columns, or no show table, still reads) → `listen/v1`, one `episode` per played episode, `raw_id` `apple-podcasts:<ZUUID>@<last played>` so an episode played again is a new line; `show`, `publisher`, `url`, `duration_s`, `played_s`, `published`, `extra.play_count`; never-played episodes counted. `logbook add apple-podcasts <copy>` reads a copy; `logbook sync apple-podcasts` reads the Mac's own store (`LOGBOOK_PODCASTS_DB` names another), opened read-only so rows in the -wal file are seen, the watermark the newest last-played time — one mapping, so the two dedupe.
- `google-takeout-youtube` file adapter: Google Takeout's `YouTube and YouTube Music/history/` — `watch-history.json` and `search-history.json` (the JSON export), streamed through `ijson` one entry at a time → `watch/v1`, source `google-takeout`: `Watched …` and `Searched for …` entries, the `header` as the service, the first subtitle as the channel, `v=` as the video id. An ad (`From Google Ads`), another activity (`Visited …`) or an entry without a time is skipped and counted; a removed video is a line without a url, counted. `logbook add "Takeout/YouTube and YouTube Music/history"` sniffs the folder or either file.
- `google-takeout-chrome` file adapter: Google Takeout's `Chrome/` folder — `History.json` (`Browser History`, one `visit` per entry from `time_usec`, `extra.transition_qualifier`; `client_id` and favicons not kept) and `Bookmarks.html` (the Netscape file, one `bookmark` per link from `ADD_DATE`, `folder` the `/`-joined `<H3>` path, parsed with the stdlib) → `browse/v1`, source `google-takeout`, `browser` `chrome`. An entry with no url or no time is skipped and counted. `logbook add Takeout/Chrome` sniffs the folder or either file.
- `safari` file adapter: Safari's `History.db` (`history_visits` joined to `history_items`, `visit_time` seconds since 2001, the columns discovered with `PRAGMA table_info` so `title` and `load_successful` are read when present and a missing one leaves the field out) → `browse/v1`, one `visit` per row, source `safari`, `raw_id` `safari:<visit_time>:<sha256(url)[:16]>` — the phone's copy and the Mac's own spell a synced visit the same way and dedupe. Opened read-only and immutable. `logbook add safari <copy>` reads a store under any name.
- `import-backup`: the Safari step runs the `safari` adapter on the copy under `inbox/<backup>/safari/` instead of reporting *copied, no adapter yet*; `ios_backup.EXTRAS` names it as an adapter source like the call log and Health.
- `google-takeout-tasks` file adapter: Google Takeout's `Tasks/Tasks.json` (every list, every task, subtasks included) → `task/v1`, source `google-takeout`, tier 2: `raw_id` `<task id>@<updated>`, `status` `done` for `completed` else `open`, `due` as the day the export's midnight instant stands for (RFC 0016 rule 4), `completed_at`, `list`, `notes`, `parent`, `extra.hidden`/`deleted`/`links`; `at` is `updated` (the export has no creation time). A task without a title is skipped and counted; a deleted one is a line, marked. `logbook add Takeout/Tasks` sniffs the folder or the file.
- `google-takeout-keep` file adapter: Google Takeout's `Keep/` folder (one JSON per note, the `.html` twins ignored, attachments beside) → `note/v1`, one line per note, source `google-takeout`, tier 2: `raw_id` `keep:<createdTimestampUsec>@<modified_at>` (Keep exports no note id; the creation instant never changes, and the edit time on the end makes an edited note a new line, RFC 0010 rule 2), `title`, `text` (a list note as `[x]`/`[ ]` lines), `modified_at`, `labels` (RFC 0010 gains the optional field), `extra.pinned`/`archived`/`trashed`/`color`/`links`; attachments as `{filename, media_type, sha256, bytes}` from the file beside the note, `path` with the bytes in the §1.1 store only with `--attachments`, a file the export left out as `{filename, media_type}` and counted. A note with no text is skipped and counted; a trashed note is a line, marked. `logbook add Takeout/Keep` sniffs the folder or one note.
- `ios-wallet` file adapter: Apple Wallet passes → `flight/v1` and `event/v1`. An air boarding pass is one `flight/v1` line per leg, evidence `tracked` (RFC 0013, which gains the producer row): the airline's IATA BCBP barcode gives carrier, flight number, route, seat, compartment and the day of the year; the year is the first plausible one among `relevantDate`, `relevantDates`, `expirationDate`, the semantic tags and any dated field, and a pass that names none is skipped and counted (`skipped_no_year`), never guessed from a file time. Only the semantic tags (`originalDepartureDate`, `originalArrivalDate`) become scheduled times; `relevantDate` is kept under `extra.relevant_date` and is the first leg's `at` when no schedule is known. A pass without a barcode is read from its primary fields' airport codes and a `flight` field, else counted. Event tickets, and bus, boat and train boarding passes, are `event/v1` lines (title, venue, the issuer as `calendar`, the pass's `locations` and front fields under `extra`, minus any field whose key names a person or a credential); store cards, coupons and generic passes are skipped and counted. The PNR, the passenger's name, the barcode and the ticket numbers never reach a line. `raw_id` is `wallet:<16 hex of pass type and serial>@<digest>`, so a pass Wallet updated is a new observation and a Flighty export of the flight supersedes it through `flights.reconcile`. Reads a `.pkpass` zip, an unpacked `.pkpass` folder, a `pass.json` or a folder of any of them; UTF-8 and UTF-16, a trailing comma forgiven. `logbook add wallet <path>`; `adapters.named("wallet")`.
- `easypark` file adapter: the EasyPark app's `recentparkings_<user>.json` → one `trip/v1` line per recently used area (`from` with description, signage code, operator and coordinates; `at` the last-used time in Apple's epoch; `raw_id` `easypark:<area id>@<at>`), oldest first; a placeholder without a time and a record with unusable coordinates are skipped and counted. `import-backup` copies the file out of `AppDomain-net.easypark.app` (a folder source whose glob names the file) and runs it.
- `wispr-flow` file adapter: the Wispr Flow dictation app's Core Data store (`ZTRANSCRIPTION`: Apple-epoch start and end, the recogniser's text, the model's tidied text, the typed text, status, origin, language, the app it was typed into) → one `transcript/v1` line per dictation, the text as a `text/plain` attachment through the `store` callback and never inline, `raw_id` `wispr-flow:<uuid>`, `participants` empty (one speaker), tier 3 by default (`--tier` overrides); rows without text or a start are skipped and counted, an end before its start dropped and counted. The recordings beside the store are not read. `logbook add wispr <file>`; `import-backup` copies the store out of `AppDomain-com.wispr.flowapp` and runs it.
- `flighty` reads the app's own store: `MainFlightyDatabase.db` from an iPhone backup (`Flight` joined to the owner's `UserFlight`, `Airport`, `Airline` and `Ticket`; gate and runway original and actual times as Unix seconds, estimates not kept; terminals, gates, aircraft, seat, cabin, reason; `isCancelled`; an actual arrival airport other than the scheduled one as `diverted_to`; the origin's date in the store's zone when the shipped table does not know the airport; the `pnr` column never read). The mapping, the `extra` keys and the `raw_id` digest are the export's, so a flight in both the backup and a CSV export is one observation and the second appends nothing, and one that carries more is a new observation the merge folds in. `sniff` knows the store by its tables; `import-backup` copies it out of `AppDomain-com.flightyapp.flighty` and runs it.
- `import-backup`: Wallet runs. `ios_backup.Source.files` names a folder source — every file matching a glob under a folder in the backup (`Library/Passes/Cards/*/pass.json`, HomeDomain), copied below the source's inbox folder with its path, the adapter run on the folder; the dry run prints how many files and bytes. `ios-wallet` follows `ios-notes` in `SOURCES`.
- `twitter` file adapter: the X app's direct-message stores (`com.atebits.tweetie.databases/v1/<account id>/<account id>-dmv2.db` of the group.com.atebits.Tweetie2 container in a backup, one per signed-in account) → `message/v1`, one line per message entry at its own timestamp: `raw_id` the entry's UUID; `chat` the conversation — `direct` when its id is two account ids, named after the other account (the owner's nickname for them, else the screen name), `group` otherwise, named by its custom title; `from_me` the store's own flag; the sender `{handle, <screen name>}` with the nickname as `name` when the store knows the user, else `{provider_id, <account id>}`, never resolved; `text` the plain text; an attachment gives `media_kind` (`link`, `image`, `video`, `gif`, `other`) with the store's own type string under `extra`; the account under `extra.account` (the sender of any entry flagged the owner's, else the id in the file's name); a status other than `Sent` under `extra.status`. Informational entries, entries with neither text nor an attachment, and undated ones are skipped and counted. `run` takes a store or a folder and reads every account's. `import-backup` gains the source `twitter`, last.
- `line` file adapter: LINE's `Line.sqlite` (`Library/Application Support/PrivateStore/P_<account>/Messages/Line.sqlite` of the group.com.linecorp.line container in a backup) → `message/v1`, one line per message at its own timestamp: `raw_id` the server's message id (`pk<row>` for one never sent, counted); `chat` the LINE chat — `direct` for a user's `u…` id with the user's name as the owner spells it, `group` for a `c…` id with the group's name when `UnifiedGroup.sqlite` is beside the store; a row without a sender is the owner's; the sender a contact hint, never resolved — `{phone}` when `Contacts.sqlite` (the app's own domain, `…/Contacts Syncing/`) pairs the user's mid with a number, else `{handle, <mid>}`, with the custom name else the profile name, the mid and the address-book name under `extra`. Text and unknown content types that carry text are `text` (`extra.content_type`); images, video, audio (`voice`), files, stickers, locations, contacts and links carry `media_kind`. Calls (content type 6) are skipped and counted as calls; a row with neither text nor a known media type, or without a timestamp, too. `run` takes the store or a folder and finds the companions beside the store or anywhere below the folder. `import-backup` gains the source `line` (three files by pattern — the account's id is in the path — the two companions copied first, beside the store), after `beeper`.
- `beeper` file adapter: Beeper's `BeeperStore.sqlite` (the group.beeper.chat.ios container in a backup) → `message/v1`, what the phone's Matrix cache holds in clear: every cached event with a body — its decrypted copy (`clearEvent`) when the app had one, else an unencrypted content — then every room's last message in clear when its event was not already logged (`extra.from_last_message`, counted). Encrypted events without a decrypted copy, reactions, redactions and redacted events, membership and bridge state are skipped and counted; the Megolm sessions are never touched. `raw_id` the Matrix event id; `chat` the room (`direct` when one-on-one, the room's display name) with `extra.network` naming the bridge (whatsapp, telegram, instagram, facebook); the owner is the member of the most rooms, `from_me` for their events and for a double-puppeted copy of their own message; the sender is a contact hint from the member row, never resolved: `{phone}` when the bridge gave the number, `{email}` when an address, else `{handle, <matrix id>}`, the contact's preferred name else the network's, the Matrix id under `extra.matrix_user`. Text, notices and emotes carry `text`; images, video, audio (`voice` when flagged), files, stickers and locations carry `media_kind` with the file name and media type under `extra` (the bytes are not in the store). `import-backup` gains the source `beeper`, after `splitwise`. `logbook/adapters/keyed_archive.py` decodes NSKeyedArchiver plists (dictionaries, arrays, strings, data, custom classes by name, cycles) for any adapter whose rows hold them.
- `splitwise` file adapter: Splitwise's `database.sqlite` (`Library/Application Support/database.sqlite` of the app's domain in a backup) → `transaction/v1`, one line per expense the owner is part of, at the expense's date: `amount` the owner's side (an expense's owed share, negative; a payment as what the owner paid, negative, or received, positive), `currency`, `merchant` the description, `category` the category name as the app shows it, `account` the group's name, `note`; the total cost, the owner's shares, every member's shares with a source-native ref (`{email}`, else `{phone}`, else `{provider_id}`, never resolved) and the name the app shows, who created it, the creation method and a deleted flag under `extra`. The owner is the person whose email is in `owner_emails` (`logbook.json`); without a match the person on the most expenses is taken and that is counted and said. An expense the owner is not a member of, or without a date, is skipped and counted. `import-backup` gains the source `splitwise`, after `copilot`. The `owner_emails` hint `add` prints now names the adapter and what it does without them.
- `copilot` file adapter: Copilot Money's `CopilotDB.sqlite` (the `database/CopilotDB.sqlite` of the group.com.copilot.production container in a backup) → `transaction/v1`, one line per row of `Transactions`: `amount` the row's amount negated (Copilot keeps Plaid's convention, debits positive), `currency`, `merchant` the owner's override else the cleaned name, `category` Copilot's own slug when set, `account` the account id, `status` from the pending flag, `note` the owner's note; the type (`regular`, `income`, `internal_transfer`), recurring flag and id, the bank's original string, Plaid's category strings, tags, the parent of a split and a deleted flag under `extra`. `at` is the stored instant as UTC (Copilot stores the day's local midnight as a UTC instant) and `date` the day of it in the record's zone. A row without a date is skipped and counted; one the owner or the bank deleted is a line with `extra.deleted`. `import-backup` gains the source `copilot`, after `apple-reminders`.
- `apple-reminders` file adapter: an iPhone's Reminders stores (`Container_v1/Stores/Data-<UUID>.sqlite` under the group.com.apple.reminders container, one per account) → `task/v1` (RFC 0016), one line per reminder at its creation time: `title`, `status` (`open`/`done`), `completed_at`, `due` (an all-day reminder's day as `YYYY-MM-DD`; a timed one as the instant the phone showed it at, since a reminder without a zone keeps its wall clock in `ZDUEDATE` as if it were UTC and the real instant in `ZDISPLAYDATEDATE`), `list`, `notes`, `parent` (the source id of the reminder a subtask sits under), `priority` (Apple's 1–4 high, 5 medium, 6–9 low), `modified_at`; the flag, the zone, a start, the list's group and the store's file name under `extra`. `raw_id` is `reminders:<uuid>@<last modified>` (rule 2), so a reminder completed or edited after an import is a new line and an unchanged one appends nothing. A reminder marked for deletion is a line with `extra.deleted`; one without a title (it lives only in an opaque title document) or without a creation date is skipped and counted. `run` takes one store or a folder and reads every `Data-*.sqlite` below it; `logbook add reminders <folder>`. Tier 2. `import-backup` gains the source `apple-reminders` (`--only reminders`), after `ios-notes`; `ios_backup.Source` now takes a pattern as its path (`Data-*.sqlite`): every file of the domain that matches is copied beside the first with its `-wal`/`-shm`, the dry run lists them all, and the adapter runs on the copies' folder.
- `mail` file adapter: an mbox mailbox — Google Takeout's `Mail/` folder (`All mail Including Spam and Trash.mbox` and the per-label files), or any mbox file or folder of them, sniffed by the `From <id> <date>` separator line — → `mail/v1`, one line per message, streamed line by line with one message in memory at a time so a multi-gigabyte export is never read whole. mboxrd `>From ` lines are unescaped; nested multipart is walked for the first `text/plain` leaf, else the first `text/html` leaf stripped by a stdlib-only `strip_html` (scripts, styles, head and comments dropped, block tags and `<br>` as newlines, entities decoded); a part whose charset Python does not know or whose bytes do not obey it is decoded with replacement characters and flagged, never dropped. Every leaf with a file name, a `Content-Disposition: attachment` or a non-text type is an attachment, named by digest always and stored in `attachments/<sha256>` (`Logbook.attach`) only with `--attachments`. `logbook add mail <file|folder> [--account EMAIL] [--attachments] [--only-labels A,B] [--skip-labels A,B] [--since DAY|RFC3339] [--tier 1|2|3]`: `--account` prefixes every `raw_id` and counts as one of the owner's addresses; the label filters match any label, case-insensitively, and count what they drop (`skipped … by label`); `--since` takes a day (the record's local midnight) or an instant; `--tier` applies to the whole run. `direction` reads `owner_emails` from `logbook.json` (a new optional key, preserved like every unknown key; `add` prints a hint when it is absent and no `--account` is given). Counts: `without a Message-ID, keyed by digest`, `timed by the mbox separator (no Date header)`, `with the body taken from HTML`, `with undecodable bytes replaced`, `attachments referenced, not stored` / `attachments stored`; a message with neither a `Date` header nor a readable separator date is skipped and counted (`without a date`). `logbook add <file.mbox>` and `logbook add ~/Takeout/Mail` reach it by sniff. `show` prints a mail line as `✉ subject — from → to (n attachments)`, the people by their resolution labels, else the header's names, else the addresses, the owner's own mail as `me`, never the body; `show --raw` prints the addresses as given and the body indented under the row. `add`'s `--tier` help no longer says transcript only. Tested on a synthetic Takeout mbox (Oslo persona, `example.org` addresses): a plain reply with escaped `From ` lines, sent mail, an HTML-only newsletter, a nested multipart message with two attachments, a message without a `Message-ID`, one without a `Date` and with an unknown charset; the mapping, the filters, the store, the dedupe on re-add, the schema, streaming (a run that fails if the file is read whole), the CLI flags and their refusal on another adapter, and `show` with and without resolutions.
- `apple-health` file adapter: an iPhone's `healthdb_secure.sqlite` (`samples` with Apple-epoch dates and `data_type`; `quantity_samples`, `category_samples`, `workouts`; `objects` → `data_provenances` for the device's product type and zone; `unit_strings` for the entered unit; `sources` from the `healthdb.sqlite` beside it) → `health-sample/v1`. Data types 3, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12, 63, 118, 183 and every row of `workouts`; quantities converted from HealthKit's canonical units (heart rate count/s → bpm, HRV s → ms; the entered quantity and unit kept under `extra.original`); every optional table and column found through PRAGMA. Skips and counts placeholder dates, rows without a date or value, spans that end before they start, unknown types and sleep stages, and heart-rate readings over the cap. `logbook add health <file>` or `add apple-health <file>` names it (`adapters.named` now resolves `calls`, `contacts`, `health` as `import-backup --only` does); `logbook add <file>` recognises the store by content. Tests build the store in the test; no database is in the repository.
- `import-backup`: Health runs. `healthdb.sqlite` is copied first (reported *copied, read beside healthdb_secure.sqlite*), then `healthdb_secure.sqlite`, and `apple-health` runs on the copy with the companion beside it; the encrypted-backup test plants a synthetic store and expects its lines. Safari alone is still *copied, no adapter yet*.
- `ais` adapter and `logbook sync ais` (ADR 0018, ADR 0017): the positions of every registered vessel with an MMSI, heard on aisstream.io's websocket (`LOGBOOK_AISSTREAM_KEY`; the `websockets` package as the `openlogbook[ais]` extra, imported only inside `pull`; `LOGBOOK_AISSTREAM_LISTEN_S`, default 60, sets the listening window) → `location/v1` with `subject` = the asset id, `tracker` `aisstream`, `raw_id` `aisstream:<mmsi>:<unix seconds>`, speed in m/s from knots, AIS "not available" sentinels left out, class A and class B reports only. The same module reads a saved stream (one message per line) for `logbook add`, through the same `draft`. `--since` filters what was heard; `--dry-run` writes nothing; a refused key is one line and exit 1, never the key.
- `adsb` adapter and `logbook sync adsb`: the current state of every registered aircraft with an icao24 from the OpenSky Network's `states/all` (anonymous by default; `LOGBOOK_OPENSKY_USER`/`LOGBOOK_OPENSKY_PASS` optional, sent only in the Authorization header) → `location/v1` with `subject`, `tracker` `opensky`, `raw_id` `opensky:<icao24>:<time_position>`, so a parked aircraft's unchanged fix is one line poll after poll. The same module reads a saved response for `logbook add`.
- `sync`: an adapter whose `pull` takes `assets` gets the record's registry; one that sets `GROUP_MARKS` keeps a watermark per group in `state/<name>.json` (`groups`, one per asset, never moved backwards) beside `since`. `add` passes the registry to a file adapter that takes it. `SKIP_PHRASES` gains `skipped_unknown_subject` and `skipped_not_a_position`.
- `flighty` file adapter: a Flighty CSV export (`logbook add flights <export.csv>`, `add flighty`, or sniffed by its header) → `flight/v1`, evidence `tracked`, one line per row. Only `Date, Airline, Flight, From, To` are needed; every other column is optional and its absence leaves the field absent. `Airline` may be ICAO or IATA, `Flight` may repeat the carrier; wall-clock times with no offset are converted with the origin's zone (departure, take-off) and the destination's or the diversion's (landing, arrival); an airport the table does not know keeps its times as text under `extra.<field>_local` and is counted; an arrival before its departure is kept and counted. Gates, terminals, seat, cabin and reason go under `extra`; the PNR and the Notes never. `raw_id` is the row's Flighty id with a digest of the mapped row, so an unchanged re-export appends nothing and an export that later carries the actual times is a new observation that supersedes the first.
- `ios-calls` file adapter: an iPhone's `CallHistory.storedata` (Core Data; `ZCALLRECORD` with `ZADDRESS`, `ZDATE` in Apple-epoch seconds, `ZDURATION`, `ZORIGINATED`, `ZANSWERED`, `ZCALLTYPE`, `ZSERVICE_PROVIDER`, `ZUNIQUE_ID`, every other column PRAGMA shows kept under `extra` with the `Z` prefix dropped, blobs and nulls left out) → `call/v1`, one line per row, streamed from a read-only immutable connection. `raw_id` is `ZUNIQUE_ID`, else the row id (counted); the counterparty goes through the shared `phone.normalise` with `LOGBOOK_DIAL_PREFIX` as `ios-contacts` does (a national spelling that cannot be completed is kept and flagged `extra.unnormalised`), an address with `@` is an email, anything else a handle, a blob address is decoded, a withheld number gives no counterparty and is counted; `service` from the provider bundle id (`com.apple.Telephony` → `cellular`, `com.apple.FaceTime` → `facetime`/`facetime-audio` by call type, a third-party CallKit app by the last part of its id) else from `ZCALLTYPE`, the raw provider under `extra.service_provider`, an unknown type under `extra.call_type`; `answered` is the phone's flag, `end` set only when answered and the duration is above zero; placeholder dates (before 1900) and dateless rows are skipped and counted, as `ios-calendar` does. `logbook add <file>` sniffs it (a `ZCALLRECORD` table); `logbook add ios-calls <file>` names it, and `add <adapter> <file|folder>...` now works for any file adapter by NAME, not only `transcript`. `import-backup` runs it on the call log an encrypted backup carries, copied out as `ios-calls/CallHistory.storedata` (was `calls/`, reported as "no adapter yet"); Health and Safari are still copied without an adapter. `show` prints a call as `← who, 7 min, cellular` / `→ who, no answer`, the counterparty by its resolution when one exists. Tests on a synthetic store built by the test: incoming, outgoing and missed, FaceTime video and audio against cellular and a WhatsApp call, the epoch conversion, a blob address, the dial prefix, unmapped columns under `extra`, dedupe on re-import, the CLI by name and by sniff, the encrypted backup round trip, and a property test over random rows.
- `import-backup` reads an encrypted iOS backup (`IsEncrypted` with a `BackupKeyBag`). `logbook/ios_backup_crypto.py` implements the publicly documented keybag format: TLV records; the passcode key by PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 over `DPSL`/`DPIC` then PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA1 over `SALT`/`ITER` (a keybag from before iOS 10.2, without `DPSL`/`DPIC`, does the SHA-1 pass alone); class keys unwrapped per RFC 3394 with the passcode key (`WRAP` bit 2; a device-wrapped class, bit 1, stays unavailable); `ManifestKey` and each row's `EncryptionKey` (in the `file` column's NSKeyedArchiver plist) as a 4-byte protection class plus a 40-byte wrapped key; files and `Manifest.db` AES-256-CBC with a zero IV, PKCS#7 padding stripped or the manifest's `Size` honoured, decrypted in 1 MiB chunks, never whole in memory. The password comes only from `LOGBOOK_BACKUP_PASSWORD` — never a flag, never printed, never in a message or a file; the keybag is checked first, so a wrong password exits 2 before any file is touched. `Manifest.db` is decrypted into `<root>/inbox/ios-backup-<udid>/` and every copy is decrypted on the way to the same layout as for an unencrypted backup, so every adapter runs unchanged; each copy is checked against the plaintext size. Stores only an encrypted backup carries (`ios_backup.EXTRAS`) are copied out with no adapter yet and say so: `health/healthdb_secure.sqlite`, `health/healthdb.sqlite`, `calls/CallHistory.storedata` (now `ios-calls/`, with an adapter, see above), `safari/History.db` (`--only health,calls,safari` names them). The inbox gains `copies.json` for every backup, encrypted or not: per copy its domain, path, copy, bytes, `encrypted` and `protection_class`, merged over earlier runs of other sources, never a key. `cryptography` is the optional extra `openlogbook[encrypted]`, imported lazily; an encrypted backup without it prints one line with `pip install "openlogbook[encrypted]"` and exits 2; the unencrypted path has no new dependency. Tested on a synthetic backup built in the test — a keybag from a test password with ten iterations, class and file keys wrapped, `Manifest.db`, `sms.db`, an attachment, a Health and a Safari store encrypted — proving the round trip byte for byte, the wrong-password refusal, the missing-extra message and the older keybag; never a real backup.
- `gcal` live adapter: `logbook sync gcal` pulls Google Calendar's private iCal feeds (`LOGBOOK_GCAL_URLS`, comma-separated, each optionally `name=url`; stdlib urllib, fetched whole) → `event/v1` (RFC 0009) through the ics adapter's own reader, now exposed as `ics.lines(text, name, counts, timezone)`, so an event imported from a calendar export and the same event pulled by URL are the same line with the same `raw_id` (`<UID>@<LAST-MODIFIED>`) and `source` (`ics`) and are deduped (ADR 0017); recurring masters and materialised occurrences, all-day and floating times (in the record's zone, which `sync` now passes to a `pull` that takes `timezone`) are unchanged. The feed's X-WR-CALNAME names the calendar as for a file; a given name applies only to a feed without one. The watermark is the newest last-modified time seen; each pull keeps the events changed since a lookback (24 h, `LOGBOOK_GCAL_LOOKBACK_H`) before it and the dedupe absorbs the overlap; a first sync reads everything; `--since` is used as given. `sync` reports seen and new per calendar (a live adapter's optional `group(draft)`; `append_many` gains a `skipped` callback for the drafts it deduped) and, on `--dry-run`, seen per calendar. A calendar that fails (an HTTP error, an unreachable host, a body that is not a calendar) is one line on stderr naming the calendar by its given name or a short hash of its URL, never the URL; the other calendars are still pulled and written, the watermark is kept, the exit is 1 (a live adapter's optional `failed` list). The URLs never appear in the config's repr or any output.
- `imessage` live adapter (#85): `logbook sync imessage` reads the Mac's own `~/Library/Messages/chat.db` (`LOGBOOK_IMESSAGE_DB` names another store, `~` expanded), opened `mode=ro` and only `immutable=1` when that fails, never written and never locked against Messages.app → `message/v1` (RFC 0008) through the file adapter's own mapping, now exposed as `imessage.lines`, so a message imported from the phone backup and the same message read on the Mac have the same line and `raw_id` (the guid) and are deduped; the tapback and group-event skips, the typedstream body, the nanosecond/second dates and the media rule are unchanged. Attachments resolve by the stored paths under the `Attachments` folder beside the store, else the Mac's own `~/Library/Messages/Attachments`; `LOGBOOK_IMESSAGE_HASH_MEDIA=0` skips the hashing as for `add`. The watermark is the newest message date; each pull starts a lookback (24 h, `LOGBOOK_IMESSAGE_LOOKBACK_H`) before it, a first sync with no watermark starts from the record's newest imessage line less the lookback, `--since` is used as given. `sync` reports lines per chat type (`in direct chats`, `in group chats`) with the skips and the attachments hashed or missing. An unreadable database is one line naming Full Disk Access for the terminal as the usual cause, exit 1. The file adapter now cuts `since` in SQL, so rows before it are neither decoded nor hashed. `sync`'s resume line names the source's item (message, point, asset) instead of always "point".
- `transcript` file adapter (#78): `logbook add transcript <file|folder> [--source <name>] [--tier 1|2|3] [--at <RFC3339>]` → `transcript/v1` (RFC 0004), one line per file. Reads JSON already in transcript/v1 shape — the interchange format for any tool, a `logbook export` line minus the chain fields, the payload kept as given, an inline `content.text` stored and replaced by its reference — WebVTT and SRT (every cue a turn; the speaker from the `<v Name>` voice tag or a leading `Name:`, else unknown; the cues' offsets give `end`), and Markdown or plain text with `Speaker: text` lines, a following line continuing the turn, and an optional front-matter block for `title`, `started_at`, `ended_at`, `participants` (`Name <email>`), a `# heading` as the title. VTT, SRT and transcript JSON are sniffed by content, so `logbook add meeting.vtt` works without the keyword; Markdown and text only through it. The file's own bytes are the SPEC §1.1 attachment: the first writer of `attachments/<sha256>` (`Logbook.attach`, `logbook/attachments.py`: write-once, a mismatching file is an error, never overwritten); `raw_id` is `<source>:<sha256>` unless the JSON brings one; the start is the front matter's, else `--at`, else a date and time in the file name (`2026-03-01T13-00-00Z …`, Zoom's `GMT20260301-130000_…`, `2026-03-01 1300 …` in the record's zone, a bare date at local midnight), else the file is skipped and counted. Tier 3 by default: a transcript carries other people's words in full. `participants` are the front matter's, then every speaker label, source-native, never resolved; `extra` carries `format`, `file`, `turns`, `speakers`, `unattributed` and `duration_s`. `transcript.draft` is the one mapping every transcript source shares (ADR 0017).
- `granola` live adapter (#78): `logbook sync granola` pulls Granola's public API v1 (`LOGBOOK_GRANOLA_KEY`, a `grn_` key from Settings → Connectors → API keys, Business and Enterprise plans; `LOGBOOK_GRANOLA_URL`, default `https://public-api.granola.ai/v1`): `GET /notes?created_after=` with cursor paging, `GET /notes/{id}?include=transcript`, and `GET /notes/{id}/transcript` paged when the note answers 413 TRANSCRIPT_TOO_LARGE. One `transcript/v1` line per recording through `transcript.draft`, so a transcript exported by hand as transcript/v1 JSON and the same one pulled here are one line: `raw_id` `granola:<note id>`, `at`/`end` the segments' span (else the calendar event's, else `created_at`), `participants` the attendees as Granola names them plus any speaker label they do not cover (never resolved), `content` the segments as JSON under `attachments/`, `source_uri` the note's web url, `extra.folders`. Granola's AI summary is a model's words, so it is never `payload.summary`: it is a second line, `note/v1`, source `granola`, tier 2, `raw_id` `granola:<note id>:summary`, `extra.derived = true` and `extra.derived_from` the transcript line's id — the one already in the record when a hand export came first (`Index.line_id`, asked through the new `lookup` callback), else the one this pull mints (`append_many` now keeps an `id` a draft brings); `LOGBOOK_GRANOLA_SUMMARIES=0` skips it. The watermark is the recording's end; `resume` looks back 24 h (`LOGBOOK_GRANOLA_LOOKBACK_H`), a first sync continues from the record's newest granola transcript, `--since` is used as given. Every request is retried once on a connection failure, a timeout, 429 or a 5xx, then the sync exits 1 with a message naming the request (a 401 names the variable) and nothing has been written: the whole pull is fetched before the first line is yielded, attachments are stored only as their line is yielded, and a dry run stores nothing. `sync --dry-run` now prints the count per kind when a source yields more than one. The key is sent only in the `Authorization` header, never in a URL, never printed. Tested against a fake of the API serving synthetic Oslo notes; no network.
- ADR 0017: backfill and live share one mapping — every source adapter has a file mode and, where the source offers it, a live mode, both producing identical lines with the same `raw_id`, so backfilled and live lines dedupe; the rule for every adapter from now on, `dawarich` and `imessage` the existing examples.
- `ics` file adapter: any iCalendar file (RFC 5545) — a Google Calendar export or Takeout's `Calendar/` folder, an Apple Calendar export, a saved feed; sniffed by content, so any extension — or a folder of them → `event/v1` (RFC 0009), one line per VEVENT with the same shape `ios-calendar` gives the same event: `at`/`end` from DTSTART/DTEND (or DURATION) in UTC, `tz` the DTSTART's TZID when the zone database knows it (a `/mozilla.org/…/Europe/Oslo` prefix is skipped over; an unknown one is read in the record's zone with the text under `extra.tzid`), floating times in the record's zone, a VALUE=DATE start an all-day event at local midnight ending at the DTEND or the next midnight; `title`, `notes`, `location` (GEO under `extra.location`), `status`, `calendar` from X-WR-CALNAME (else the file name), organizer and attendees as `mailto:` email refs with CN and PARTSTAT; a master's RRULE text as `recurrence` (EXDATE/RDATE under `extra`), a RECURRENCE-ID exception's `recurrence_of`; occurrences never expanded. `raw_id` is `<UID>@<LAST-MODIFIED, else DTSTAMP>` (rule 5), an exception keyed `<UID>/<RECURRENCE-ID>@…`. VEVENTs without a DTSTART, with an unreadable one or one before 1900, or without a UID are skipped and counted, as are VTODO and VJOURNAL blocks. The parser is stdlib only and tolerant: line unfolding, BEGIN/END nesting (an unmatched END is ignored, an open block closes at the end of the file), quoted and multi-valued parameters, TEXT escapes, CRLF or LF, a BOM; what it does not know it passes over. `logbook/adapters/takeout/calendar.py` is the Takeout package's calendar sibling of `location`, delegating to `ics`.
- `import-backup <folder> [--dry-run] [--only names]`: every phone source from an unencrypted iOS backup folder in one go. `logbook/ios_backup.py` reads `Manifest.db` (opened read-only and immutable, so not even a journal appears beside it) and `Manifest.plist`, locates each adapter's store by domain and path — `AddressBook.sqlitedb` → `ios-contacts`, `ContactsV2.sqlite` → `whatsapp-contacts`, `ChatStorage.sqlite` with `Message/` → `whatsapp`, `sms.db` with `Library/SMS/Attachments/` → `imessage`, `Calendar.sqlitedb` → `ios-calendar`, `NoteStore.sqlite` → `ios-notes` — and in that order copies each store with its `-wal`/`-shm` siblings and its media into `<root>/inbox/ios-backup-<udid>/<source>/` under the original names, checks every copy by size, and runs the adapter on the copy (never on the backup, which is never written). A media path that would leave its folder is never copied. Per source: found or not found (a row whose file is missing says so), lines added, skips and notes as `add` reports them; then `verify`. `--dry-run` lists the files and sizes and writes nothing; `--only contacts,whatsapp` limits the run (the fixed order stays). An encrypted backup (`IsEncrypted`) is refused with a one-line message naming Finder's checkbox; a folder without `Manifest.db` exits 2. `LOGBOOK_DIAL_PREFIX` reaches the adapters as with `add`, and a hint is printed when it is unset. The registry gains `adapters.named(name)`.
- `google-takeout-photos` file adapter, the second Takeout sub-adapter: the unzipped `Takeout/Google Photos/` folder (or the Takeout root that holds it, or one album folder) → `photo/v1` (RFC 0002), one line per media file from its JSON sidecar: `at` from `photoTakenTime` (falling back to `creationTime`, counted), `raw_id` the id in the sidecar's Google Photos url else `<album>/<file>`, `provenance` through `googlePhotosOrigin` as a data rule (ADR 0011: `mobileUpload` → `camera`, `fromSharedAlbum` → `received`, else `other`), `lat`/`lon` from `geoData` (then `geoDataExif`) when non-zero, the caption, the album and the tagged names as `extra.people` `{kind: "name", value}` refs, never resolved and never in `payload.people`. A live-photo pair (a still and a video with one stem) is one `camera` line with the motion half under `extra.live_video`. Sidecars are matched by Google's naming (`<file>.json`, `<file>.supplemental-metadata.json`, the truncated and `(1)`-numbered variants), then by `title`, then by a unique prefix; a sidecar with no file is counted and a file with no sidecar is still a line, timed by the file and flagged `extra.no_sidecar`. Media v1 rule as for WhatsApp: the file is hashed into `extra.media` (`sha256`, `bytes`, `local_path`, `media_type`), never copied; `LOGBOOK_TAKEOUT_HASH_MEDIA=0` skips the hashing. `logbook add <folder>` now hands a folder to an adapter that claims it as a whole before walking its files.
- `dawarich` live adapter: `logbook sync dawarich` pulls `GET /api/v1/points` from your own Dawarich server (`LOGBOOK_DAWARICH_URL`, `LOGBOOK_DAWARICH_KEY` as a Bearer header; stdlib urllib, paged until an empty page, yielded oldest first) → `location/v1` (RFC 0001) through the file adapter's own mapping, now exposed as `dawarich.draft`, so a point already imported from an export has the same line and `raw_id` and is deduped. The watermark is the newest point time; each pull starts a lookback (24 h, `LOGBOOK_DAWARICH_LOOKBACK_H`) before it for late batch uploads, and a first sync with no watermark starts from the record's newest dawarich location line (`Index.newest`) less the lookback. Rows without usable coordinates, timestamp or tracker are skipped and counted. `sync` now reports how many pulled lines were already in the record and what the adapter skipped, never moves a watermark backwards, and exits 1 on a malformed response. The registry keeps one file and one live adapter per `NAME`. The shared mapping now drops a non-finite altitude/speed/heading and a heading above 360.
- `whatsapp-contacts` file adapter: an iPhone's WhatsApp ContactsV2.sqlite (also the hashed file in an unencrypted Finder/iTunes backup) → one `resolution/v1` alias line per contact with a linked-device id and a phone number that normalises: `ref` is the lid spelled as the `whatsapp` adapter spells a `@lid` sender, `alias_of` the phone through the shared `adapters/phone.py` (`LOGBOOK_DIAL_PREFIX` as for `ios-contacts`), `label` the name WhatsApp shows, `raw_id` `alias:handle:<lid>` so a later backup appends nothing already aliased. Nothing is minted: the phone's entity comes from the owner's contacts import, before or after. Rows without a lid, without a usable phone, or with a lid already seen are skipped and counted. Opened read-only and immutable, no network (#58).

### Readers and commands
- `logbook serve [--port N]` (`logbook/serve.py`, [docs/serve.md](docs/serve.md)): the record read in a browser, on this machine only. Server-rendered HTML with one inline stylesheet, no script, no font, no image and no link to anywhere but its own paths (a `Content-Security-Policy` of `default-src 'none'` says so to the browser too), every page from the index and the readers the commands use, nothing written. `/day/YYYY-MM-DD` is the Day (`day.read`) as a timeline — the nights, the country, one row per stay, stop, move, flight or run aboard an asset, each row's attachments and company under a toggle (`?open=1` opens them all), what is unplaced, the health line, the sources; `/days?from=&to=` one row per day (`days.read`; the last fortnight without a window; a form and earlier/later links), each a link to its Day; `/trips?year=` the year's trips (`trips.trips`), each linking to its days; `/places`, `/assets` (with the last fix of each, `asset_status.read`) and `/gaps?since=` (`gaps.report`). The server binds 127.0.0.1 and refuses any other host (`--host 0.0.0.0`, `::`, a LAN address, `localhost`) with exit 2 before a socket is opened, and never resolves a name, not even its own. A Day answers in the time `logbook day` takes, through the index, whatever the record's length. Tests: the refusal of every other host, that no page references a URL outside itself (every URL-bearing attribute, the inline style, the headers), the pages on the synthetic persona, a 404 and a 400, that serving writes nothing, and one request over the loopback socket. The text helpers of `logbook/day.py` and `logbook/days.py` the pages reuse are public now (`day.span_text`, `days.night_text`, …).
- `logbook infer keepers [--dry-run]` (`logbook/keepers.py`, producer `keeper-inference`): a `memory` keeper for every photo line its library marked a favourite (`extra.favorite`; the `immich` adapter now keeps `isFavorite` there, `apple-photos` reads as `ios-photos`) and an `art` keeper for every photo in an album named Art (`extra.album` or `extra.albums`, case aside); through `append_many`, so a re-run appends nothing and a retracted keeper is never written again. `logbook keepers [--since DAY] [--until DAY] [--lane memory|art] [--json]` lists the keeper lines standing by day, with the photo's file name. `show <day>` prints the day's keepers under the date as `hero  <file names>` (memory first, `(art)` marked) and each keeper line as `hero photo (<lane>): <file>`.
- `logbook show person|asset|place <name> [--json]` (`logbook/pages.py`): the pages, read from the whole record (the days the owner's track covers). `show person <name>` takes a label (case aside), a unique first name or an entity id and prints the arc: the refs the person resolves from, first and last contact, days together per year, the places shared, open commitments (a placeholder that says `commitment/v1` lines are not read yet), the last ten shared stays with how each was decided, and under `--json` the proposed evidence count and the line ids. `show asset <id>`: the trips aboard (from `trips`), nights, stays and hours aboard, the people aboard, and the summary of the asset's own track when the record holds one (points, stays, moves, distance, first and last fix, the first and last line). `show place <name>`: visits, hours, first and last, per year, the people met there, the photos taken there (photo lines inside a stay at the place), the last ten visits. `show <day>` is unchanged.
- `logbook trips [--year YYYY | --since YYYY-MM-DD --until YYYY-MM-DD] [--airports FILE] [--json]` (`logbook/trips.py`): runs of consecutive days whose overnight stay is outside every home region (places.json, kind `home`) or in transit, from one reading of the window. Each trip: a derived id (`trip:<first day>:<last day>`), its first and last day, the return day (`until`, the day after the last night), nights and nights in transit, the route in night order (the named place, else `near <place>` for a named place within 25 km, else the nearest large airport's name as a coarse city, else coordinates; repeats collapse), the named places visited, the people confirmed present (the with module) between the first day's midnight and the end of the return day, every flight dated inside it and the flights in (dated the first day) and out (dated the return day), `asset` when every night was aboard one asset, and the line ids it rests on. A run made only of in-transit nights is not a trip. Without a place of kind `home` there are no trips, and the command says why. ADR 0019: a trip is never a line, only a reader's output, until the captain names it, and the name is a `note/v1`.
- `logbook rollup places|people`: the two rollups that read company through the with module. `places`: per named place and per asset (the owner's stays aboard it), per year — stays, hours, first and last visit (the stay's start and end day), an asset's nights aboard, and the people confirmed present with their days and lines. `people`: per resolved person, per year — days together (days with confirmed evidence, by the evidence's own day), the last real contact (the last such day), the places shared (a named place, `aboard <asset>`, else coordinates), confirmed and proposed evidence counts and the lines; names that resolve to no person are listed apart under `unresolved`.
- `logbook/present.py`, the "with" module: who was with the owner at a stay. `present(stay, lines, identities)` lists one entry per piece of evidence — a person id when the ref or name resolves through the record's resolution lines (RFC 0006; `resolve.identities_from` gives each ref its entity id and label), else the name or address as the source spelled it — with a confidence, a status and a reason: an attendee of a calendar entry whose span overlaps the stay (confirmed, 0.8 accepted, 0.6 tentative or unanswered, declined absent; `attendee of <title>`), a participant of a transcript recorded inside it (confirmed, 0.9, by email, phone, provider id, else the name against the labels; `spoke in <title>`), a note written inside it that says `with <Name>` (and `<Name>`; confirmed, 1.0; `note says with <name>`), and a face the library tagged in a photo taken inside it (`provider_id` ref `<library>:<person id>`; proposed, 0.5; `face in <file>`). A circle page is listed as a source and names nobody yet. `company` merges the evidence per person, confirmed first, with the sources, reasons and line ids.
- `logbook rollup countries|flights|nights [--year YYYY | --since YYYY-MM-DD --until YYYY-MM-DD] [--airports FILE] [--json]` (`logbook/rollup.py`): the record summed up per year from one reading of the window, clipped to the days the owner's track covers (a day outside them is nothing, not a night in transit). `countries`: days per country per year from the overnight stay, in-transit nights and nights whose country is unknown listed separately, and the method printed with the numbers — `logbook/countries.py`: a place with a `country` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, a new optional `places.json` field) whose radius holds the stay decides, else the nearest large airport within 300 km (the airports table) and its zone's country through the bundled `logbook/tables/zones.csv` (the zone database's public-domain `zone.tab`, regenerated by `scripts/make_zones.py`); coarse near borders and far from airports, and the module says so. `flights`: count, kilometres (great-circle between the airports table's coordinates), long-haul (over 3,500 km), unmeasured (an airport the table does not know), by evidence (tracked, inferred, declared), per year, from the flight lines standing (RFC 0013 rule 4), each flight listed with its route and km. `nights`: home, away and in-transit nights per year, nights aboard each asset, and the longest trip (the longest run of consecutive nights not at home); without a place of kind `home` every night is away and the rollup says so. Under `--json` every number carries the ids of the lines it came from: a stay by its first and last location line (one unbroken run), a flight by its line standing. `Index.span(kind)` gives the first and last local day with a line of a kind.
- `logbook places list|add|name|propose` (and `import-takeout`, which moved beside them: the Google Maps saved-places importer now lives at `logbook/adapters/takeout/places.py`): the named places of the record. `places.json` (`logbook/places.py`) maps a name to `{lat, lon, radius_m, kind, tags}`, `kind` one of `home`, `asset-berth`, `other` (absent is `other`; the old three-field shape still reads); home regions are the places of kind `home`, and `derive stays` now marks each night `home` (`"home": true` in the JSON, `· home` in the table) when its stay is a home place or lies in one's radius. `add <name> --lat --lon [--radius] [--kind] [--tags]` edits the file only; `name <lat,lon|stay-id> <name>` adds the place and appends one `note/v1` line, `named <lat>,<lon> as <name>`, so the naming is in the record. `propose [--since] [--until] [--top N] [--json]` is a reader: the owner's unnamed stays of the window (the whole record by default) grouped within 300 m and ranked by hours, each with its stay ids, the nearest known place and the distance, the asset it was aboard, the Google Timeline visits that overlap it when the record holds Takeout timeline lines (place id, semantic type, probability), a suggested name (the visit's semantic type, else `aboard <asset>`, else `near <place>` within 2 km), and under `lines` the ids of its first and last location line and the point count; `--write` asks for each name (Enter accepts the suggestion, `s` skips, `q` stops) and names the accepted ones as `name` does. Every segment of `derive stays --json` now carries a derived `id` (`stay:owner:20260610T1000Z@59.9200,10.7400`: kind, subject, start to the minute, centre) and `lines` (first and last location line id, points). `logbook/reading.py` is the one loader the readers share: a window's lines through the index, the settings, places and assets, the derived stays and nights, and the record's resolutions.
- `policy/import.json`, `{"disabled": [{"source", "reason"}]}`: the sources the owner switched off. `add`, `sync` and `import-backup` skip a disabled source and say so (`<name>: disabled (<reason>); skipped`), `--only` or not, before anything is copied or any variable is asked for; `logbook sources` lists every adapter this build has, file and live, with `enabled` or `disabled (<reason>)`, then any disabled name no adapter carries.
